I try to eat a bit cleaner so I don't get weighed down on the ladders. \n\nSo, yeah, I might weigh a little less than them, but I don't think it's in a bad way. It just means I'm a bit more streamlined for the work. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too thin?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really? I mean... I guess it's possible? I really don't want to sound stubborn or like I'm ignoring what everyone is saying. I know my buddies are worried, and that insurance doctor definitely made a big deal out of it... and I appreciate that they care, I really do. 

But I just... I still feel like they're kind of overreacting? Because, like I said, I'm still showing up to my jobs every single day. Um... I wouldn't say I'm *afraid* of it, exactly. I hope that's the right word to use. It's more just... I like to keep things under control?

So I usually just make sure to cut back the next day, just so it doesn't stick around. 

It's just a way of staying on track with my routine. That's pretty normal, right? I mean, a lot of guys try to watch their weight as they get older, so I'm just trying to be responsible about it. I hope you don't think I'm being too extreme or anything.

Oh, um... I know I kind of just touched on this, but... I guess if you're asking again, maybe I wasn't being totally clear? I'm sorry about that, I really want to make sure I'm answering you properly.

Um... I guess maybe a little? I mean, I don't want to sound vain or anything. It's not like I'm obsessed with my looks, I promise. It's more just... wanting to look put together, you know?

Um... yeah, I guess that's a big part of it. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm answering you right.

Um... wow, that's... that's a really interesting question. I've actually never thought about it like that before. I hope it's okay if I take a second to think about it? I just want to give you a good answer.

Does that sound reasonable to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as other people your height?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe I'm a little lighter than some guys my height? I hope it's okay to say that. I mean, a lot of the guys on the crew are pretty big, you know, they eat a lot of fast food and stuff on breaks. I just... I try to eat a bit cleaner so I don't get weighed down on the ladders. \n\nSo, yeah, I might weigh a little less than them, but I don't think it's in a bad way. It just means I'm a bit more streamlined for the work. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too thin?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really? I mean, I know I probably sound like a broken record here, and I hope you aren't getting frustrated with me for saying it again. But... like I mentioned before, I know my friends and that insurance doctor are pretty worried about how I look. I just really feel like they're overreacting. \n\nBecause if I was actually too thin or looked sick, I wouldn't be able to keep showing up to my jobs every morning and getting all my work done, right? My body would just give out. I just look like a guy who stays active and works hard. Is it okay that I see it that way? I really am trying to give you the best answers I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a possibility that you might be underestimating how thin you look?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess it's possible? I really don't want to sound stubborn or like I'm ignoring what everyone is saying. I know my buddies are worried, and that insurance doctor definitely made a big deal out of it... and I appreciate that they care, I really do. \n\nBut I just... I still feel like they're kind of overreacting? Because, like I said, I'm still showing up to my jobs every single day. I'm climbing around, painting all day, getting all my work done without any issues. If I was really as thin as they think I am, I wouldn't have the energy for all that, would I? I'm just trying to look at it logically... I hope that doesn't make me sound difficult. I'm just trying to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat less than other people?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little less? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm doing something wrong. It's just... well, most days I kind of keep it to coffee in the morning, and maybe I'll have a protein bar between paint jobs. Then just a small dinner when I get home. \n\nI just... I've found that eating a full meal during the day really feels like it would slow me down, you know? Like I'd get sluggish going up and down the ladders. So I just keep it light to stay productive. Does that seem reasonable to you? I'm just trying to do what works best for my job, and I hope you don't think I'm being unhealthy about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... I wouldn't say I'm *afraid* of it, exactly. I hope that's the right word to use. It's more just... I like to keep things under control? \n\nLike, sometimes I have to go to a client dinner, or maybe I end up eating something with bread... and if I check the scale the next morning and it's gone up even just a little bit, I do get kind of... tense, I guess? So I usually just make sure to cut back the next day, just so it doesn't stick around. \n\nIt's just a way of staying on track with my routine. That's pretty normal, right? I mean, a lot of guys try to watch their weight as they get older, so I'm just trying to be responsible about it. I hope you don't think I'm being too extreme or anything.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I know I kind of just touched on this, but... I guess if you're asking again, maybe I wasn't being totally clear? I'm sorry about that, I really want to make sure I'm answering you properly. \n\nI guess... well, if I really think about it, maybe it is a little closer to a fear than I just made it sound. Like I was saying about those client dinners, or if I end up eating bread... when I step on the scale the next morning and see that it went up, even just a tiny fraction of a pound, I get really... well, highly anxious, I guess. It really bothers me. So I immediately start cutting back my food the next day to make absolutely sure it doesn't stick. I just really, really need that number to go back down to feel okay again. \n\nI hope I'm not sounding too crazy or difficult when I say that? I'm just trying to give you the full picture, like you asked.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is related to how you want to look?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little? I mean, I don't want to sound vain or anything. It's not like I'm obsessed with my looks, I promise. It's more just... wanting to look put together, you know? \n\nSometimes, if I catch a glimpse of myself in a mirror, or... or if a client takes a photo of me standing next to one of my finished murals—which they do sometimes, for their social media or whatever—I just... well, I kind of find myself focusing on my stomach or my face. And if they don't look... sharp enough, or lean enough, it just kind of ruins the whole day for me. \n\nI just want to look professional and healthy, you know? Like I take care of myself. I hope that makes sense. Is it okay that I care about that? I feel like a lot of people want to look good in photos, right?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight because you don't want to lose your current body shape?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess that's a big part of it. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm answering you right. \n\nLike I mentioned before about the photos... when I catch myself in a mirror, or if I see one of those pictures a client takes of me next to a finished mural... I really just zero in on my stomach and my face. And if they don't look exactly the way I want—if they aren't, you know, sharp or lean enough—it honestly just dictates my mood for the entire day. I end up judging myself so harshly based on that one thing. \n\nSo, yeah, losing this shape... it would mean I'd have to feel that way all the time, and I just don't think I could handle that. I just want to feel okay about how I look, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound too superficial or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be afraid of gaining weight if you couldn't see your body?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's... that's a really interesting question. I've actually never thought about it like that before. I hope it's okay if I take a second to think about it? I just want to give you a good answer.\n\nI guess... if I really couldn't see myself at all... maybe the fear wouldn't be as bad? Because, like I was saying, so much of it is tied to what I see in the mirror or in those photos clients take. If I didn't have that visual... confirmation, I suppose, maybe I wouldn't be as anxious about it. \n\nBut then again, there's the scale, right? I'd still know the number. And I think just knowing the number went up would still make me feel... well, pretty uncomfortable. I'm sorry, I know that's not a very definitive answer. Does that make sense, though? I'm trying to be as honest as I can.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I know I probably sound like a broken record here, and I hope you aren't getting frustrated with me for saying it again. But... like I mentioned before, I know my friends and that insurance doctor are pretty worried about how I look. I just really feel like they're overreacting. \n\nBecause if I was actually too thin or looked sick, I wouldn't be able to keep showing up to my jobs every morning and getting all my work done, right? My body would just give out. I just look like a guy who stays active and works hard. Is it okay that I see it that way? I really am trying to give you the best answers I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a possibility that you might be underestimating how thin you look?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess it's possible? I really don't want to sound stubborn or like I'm ignoring what everyone is saying. I know my buddies are worried, and that insurance doctor definitely made a big deal out of it... and I appreciate that they care, I really do. \n\nBut I just... I still feel like they're kind of overreacting? Because, like I said, I'm still showing up to my jobs every single day. I'm climbing around, painting all day, getting all my work done without any issues. If I was really as thin as they think I am, I wouldn't have the energy for all that, would I? I'm just trying to look at it logically... I hope that doesn't make me sound difficult. I'm just trying to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat less than other people?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little less? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm doing something wrong. It's just... well, most days I kind of keep it to coffee in the morning, and maybe I'll have a protein bar between paint jobs. Then just a small dinner when I get home. \n\nI just... I've found that eating a full meal during the day really feels like it would slow me down, you know? Like I'd get sluggish going up and down the ladders. So I just keep it light to stay productive. Does that seem reasonable to you? I'm just trying to do what works best for my job, and I hope you don't think I'm being unhealthy about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... I wouldn't say I'm *afraid* of it, exactly. I hope that's the right word to use. It's more just... I like to keep things under control? \n\nLike, sometimes I have to go to a client dinner, or maybe I end up eating something with bread... and if I check the scale the next morning and it's gone up even just a little bit, I do get kind of... tense, I guess? So I usually just make sure to cut back the next day, just so it doesn't stick around. \n\nIt's just a way of staying on track with my routine. That's pretty normal, right? I mean, a lot of guys try to watch their weight as they get older, so I'm just trying to be responsible about it. I hope you don't think I'm being too extreme or anything.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I know I kind of just touched on this, but... I just really, really need that number to go back down to feel okay again. \n\nI hope I'm not sounding too crazy or difficult when I say that? I'm up on ladders, reaching, bending... I just want to make sure I'm in the best shape to do my work well, you know? \n\nI guess I just associate being lighter with having more energy and being more efficient. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... wow, that's a tough one. I guess... I guess it's a mix of both? I mean, I know I talk a lot about how I look, and I really hope I don't sound incredibly vain when I say that. It's just... in my line of work, people see me, they see my art, and I just want to present a good image. 

But I suppose it's also about... control? Or just keeping things in check. Like, if I have to go to a dinner with a client to discuss a mural, and maybe I have something with bread or something a little heavier than usual... if the scale goes up even just a tiny bit the next morning, I get... well, I guess "tense" is the right word. I just get a little anxious about it. So I'll usually just cut back on what I eat the next day, just to make sure it doesn't stick. 

I just like to keep things balanced, you know? I don't want things to get out of hand. Is that... is that pretty common for people to do? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting or anything.

I'm just trying to look at it logically... I hope that doesn't make me sound difficult. I'm just trying to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat less than other people?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little less? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm doing something wrong. It's just... well, most days I kind of keep it to coffee in the morning, and maybe I'll have a protein bar between paint jobs. Then just a small dinner when I get home. \n\nI just... I've found that eating a full meal during the day really feels like it would slow me down, you know? Like I'd get sluggish going up and down the ladders. So I just keep it light to stay productive. Does that seem reasonable to you? I'm just trying to do what works best for my job, and I hope you don't think I'm being unhealthy about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... I like to keep things under control? \n\nLike, sometimes I have to go to a client dinner, or maybe I end up eating something with bread... and if I check the scale the next morning and it's gone up even just a little bit, I do get kind of... tense, I guess? So I usually just make sure to cut back the next day, just so it doesn't stick around. \n\nIt's just a way of staying on track with my routine. That's pretty normal, right? I mean, a lot of guys try to watch their weight as they get older, so I'm just trying to be responsible about it. I hope you don't think I'm being too extreme or anything.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I know I kind of just touched on this, but... I guess if you're asking again, maybe I wasn't being totally clear? I'm sorry about that, I really want to make sure I'm answering you properly. \n\nI guess... well, if I really think about it, maybe it is a little closer to a fear than I just made it sound. Like I was saying about those client dinners, or if I end up eating bread... when I step on the scale the next morning and see that it went up, even just a tiny fraction of a pound, I get really... well, highly anxious, I guess. It really bothers me. So I immediately start cutting back my food the next day to make absolutely sure it doesn't stick. I just really, really need that number to go back down to feel okay again. \n\nI hope I'm not sounding too crazy or difficult when I say that? I'm just trying to give you the full picture, like you asked.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is related to how you want to look?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little? I mean, I don't want to sound vain or anything. It's not like I'm obsessed with my looks, I promise. It's more just... wanting to look put together, you know? \n\nSometimes, if I catch a glimpse of myself in a mirror, or... or if a client takes a photo of me standing next to one of my finished murals—which they do sometimes, for their social media or whatever—I just... well, I kind of find myself focusing on my stomach or my face. And if they don't look... sharp enough, or lean enough, it just kind of ruins the whole day for me. \n\nI just want to look professional and healthy, you know? Like I take care of myself. Um... honestly, I think if I truly didn't care about how I looked, I probably wouldn't be quite as strict with myself. I really do feel like they're blowing it out of proportion. \n\nI'm still putting in full days, climbing ladders, carrying heavy paint buckets... I'm getting everything done just fine. If I wasn't healthy, I wouldn't be able to keep up with the physical demands of my job, right? I'm just trying to stay in good shape so I can keep working hard. Does that sound reasonable to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as other people your height?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe I'm a little lighter than some guys my height? I hope it's okay to say that. I mean, a lot of the guys on the crew are pretty big, you know, they eat a lot of fast food and stuff on breaks. I just... I try to eat a bit cleaner so I don't get weighed down on the ladders. \n\nSo, yeah, I might weigh a little less than them, but I don't think it's in a bad way. It just means I'm a bit more streamlined for the work. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too thin?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really? I mean, I know I probably sound like a broken record here, and I hope you aren't getting frustrated with me for saying it again. But... like I mentioned before, I know my friends and that insurance doctor are pretty worried about how I look. I just really feel like they're overreacting. \n\nBecause if I was actually too thin or looked sick, I wouldn't be able to keep showing up to my jobs every morning and getting all my work done, right? My body would just give out. I just look like a guy who stays active and works hard. Is it okay that I see it that way? I really am trying to give you the best answers I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a possibility that you might be underestimating how thin you look?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess it's possible? I really don't want to sound stubborn or like I'm ignoring what everyone is saying. I know my buddies are worried, and that insurance doctor definitely made a big deal out of it... and I appreciate that they care, I really do. \n\nBut I just... I still feel like they're kind of overreacting? Because, like I said, I'm still showing up to my jobs every single day. I'm climbing around, painting all day, getting all my work done without any issues. If I was really as thin as they think I am, I wouldn't have the energy for all that, would I? I'm just trying to look at it logically... I hope that doesn't make me sound difficult. I'm just trying to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat less than other people?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little less? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm doing something wrong. It's just... well, most days I kind of keep it to coffee in the morning, and maybe I'll have a protein bar between paint jobs. Then just a small dinner when I get home. \n\nI just... I've found that eating a full meal during the day really feels like it would slow me down, you know? Like I'd get sluggish going up and down the ladders. So I just keep it light to stay productive. Does that seem reasonable to you? I'm just trying to do what works best for my job, and I hope you don't think I'm being unhealthy about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... I wouldn't say I'm *afraid* of it, exactly. I hope that's the right word to use. It's more just... I like to keep things under control? \n\nLike, sometimes I have to go to a client dinner, or maybe I end up eating something with bread... and if I check the scale the next morning and it's gone up even just a little bit, I do get kind of... tense, I guess? So I usually just make sure to cut back the next day, just so it doesn't stick around. \n\nIt's just a way of staying on track with my routine. That's pretty normal, right? I mean, a lot of guys try to watch their weight as they get older, so I'm just trying to be responsible about it. I hope you don't think I'm being too extreme or anything.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I know I kind of just touched on this, but... I guess if you're asking again, maybe I wasn't being totally clear? I'm sorry about that, I really want to make sure I'm answering you properly. \n\nI guess... well, if I really think about it, maybe it is a little closer to a fear than I just made it sound. Like I was saying about those client dinners, or if I end up eating bread... when I step on the scale the next morning and see that it went up, even just a tiny fraction of a pound, I get really... well, highly anxious, I guess. It really bothers me. So I immediately start cutting back my food the next day to make absolutely sure it doesn't stick. I just really, really need that number to go back down to feel okay again. \n\nI hope I'm not sounding too crazy or difficult when I say that? I'm just trying to give you the full picture, like you asked.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is related to how you want to look?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little? I mean, I don't want to sound vain or anything. It's not like I'm obsessed with my looks, I promise. It's more just... wanting to look put together, you know? \n\nSometimes, if I catch a glimpse of myself in a mirror, or... or if a client takes a photo of me standing next to one of my finished murals—which they do sometimes, for their social media or whatever—I just... well, I kind of find myself focusing on my stomach or my face. And if they don't look... sharp enough, or lean enough, it just kind of ruins the whole day for me. \n\nI just want to look professional and healthy, you know? Like I take care of myself. I hope that makes sense. Is it okay that I care about that? I feel like a lot of people want to look good in photos, right?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight because you don't want to lose your current body shape?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess that's a big part of it. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm answering you right. \n\nLike I mentioned before about the photos... when I catch myself in a mirror, or if I see one of those pictures a client takes of me next to a finished mural... I really just zero in on my stomach and my face. And if they don't look exactly the way I want—if they aren't, you know, sharp or lean enough—it honestly just dictates my mood for the entire day. I end up judging myself so harshly based on that one thing. \n\nSo, yeah, losing this shape... it would mean I'd have to feel that way all the time, and I just don't think I could handle that. I just want to feel okay about how I look, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound too superficial or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be afraid of gaining weight if you couldn't see your body?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's... that's a really interesting question. I've actually never thought about it like that before. I hope it's okay if I take a second to think about it? I just want to give you a good answer.\n\nI guess... if I really couldn't see myself at all... maybe the fear wouldn't be as bad? Because, like I was saying, so much of it is tied to what I see in the mirror or in those photos clients take. If I didn't have that visual... confirmation, I suppose, maybe I wouldn't be as anxious about it. \n\nBut then again, there's the scale, right? I'd still know the number. I'm just trying to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat less than other people?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little less? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm doing something wrong. It's just... well, most days I kind of keep it to coffee in the morning, and maybe I'll have a protein bar between paint jobs. Then just a small dinner when I get home. \n\nI just... I've found that eating a full meal during the day really feels like it would slow me down, you know? Like I'd get sluggish going up and down the ladders. So I just keep it light to stay productive. Does that seem reasonable to you? I'm just trying to do what works best for my job, and I hope you don't think I'm being unhealthy about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... I wouldn't say I'm *afraid* of it, exactly. I hope that's the right word to use. It's more just... I like to keep things under control? \n\nLike, sometimes I have to go to a client dinner, or maybe I end up eating something with bread... and if I check the scale the next morning and it's gone up even just a little bit, I do get kind of... tense, I guess? So I usually just make sure to cut back the next day, just so it doesn't stick around. \n\nIt's just a way of staying on track with my routine. That's pretty normal, right? I mean, a lot of guys try to watch their weight as they get older, so I'm just trying to be responsible about it. I hope you don't think I'm being too extreme or anything.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I know I kind of just touched on this, but... I guess if you're asking again, maybe I wasn't being totally clear? I'm sorry about that, I really want to make sure I'm answering you properly. \n\nI guess... well, if I really think about it, maybe it is a little closer to a fear than I just made it sound. Like I was saying about those client dinners, or if I end up eating bread... when I step on the scale the next morning and see that it went up, even just a tiny fraction of a pound, I get really... well, highly anxious, I guess. It really bothers me. So I immediately start cutting back my food the next day to make absolutely sure it doesn't stick. I just really, really need that number to go back down to feel okay again. \n\nI hope I'm not sounding too crazy or difficult when I say that? I'm just trying to give you the full picture, like you asked.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is related to how you want to look?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little? I mean, I don't want to sound vain or anything. It's not like I'm obsessed with my looks, I promise. It's more just... wanting to look put together, you know? \n\nSometimes, if I catch a glimpse of myself in a mirror, or... or if a client takes a photo of me standing next to one of my finished murals—which they do sometimes, for their social media or whatever—I just... well, I kind of find myself focusing on my stomach or my face. And if they don't look... sharp enough, or lean enough, it just kind of ruins the whole day for me. \n\nI just want to look professional and healthy, you know? Like I take care of myself. I hope that makes sense. Is it okay that I care about that? I feel like a lot of people want to look good in photos, right?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight because you don't want to lose your current body shape?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess that's a big part of it. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm answering you right. \n\nLike I mentioned before about the photos... when I catch myself in a mirror, or if I see one of those pictures a client takes of me next to a finished mural... I really just zero in on my stomach and my face. And if they don't look exactly the way I want—if they aren't, you know, sharp or lean enough—it honestly just dictates my mood for the entire day. I end up judging myself so harshly based on that one thing. \n\nSo, yeah, losing this shape... it would mean I'd have to feel that way all the time, and I just don't think I could handle that. I just want to feel okay about how I look, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound too superficial or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be afraid of gaining weight if you couldn't see your body?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's... that's a really interesting question. I've actually never thought about it like that before. I hope it's okay if I take a second to think about it? I just want to give you a good answer.\n\nI guess... if I really couldn't see myself at all... maybe the fear wouldn't be as bad? Because, like I was saying, so much of it is tied to what I see in the mirror or in those photos clients take. If I didn't have that visual... confirmation, I suppose, maybe I wouldn't be as anxious about it. \n\nBut then again, there's the scale, right? I'd still know the number. And I think just knowing the number went up would still make me feel... well, pretty uncomfortable. I'm sorry, I know that's not a very definitive answer. Does that make sense, though? I'm trying to be as honest as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be afraid of gaining weight if it didn't affect your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess if it didn't change how I looked at all... maybe it wouldn't be quite as terrifying? I mean, I hope it's okay to admit that. I just... I know I've brought this up a couple of times already, and I'm really sorry if I'm sounding like a broken record, but it always comes back to what I see. \n\nLike I said about the mirror, or those pictures my clients post of me with my murals... I just can't help but judge my entire day, my whole worth, I guess, on whether my stomach or my face looks sharp enough in that exact moment. If my appearance didn't change, maybe I wouldn't have that visual trigger to make me feel so... inadequate? \n\nBut I still think I'd worry about the number, just because I want to be healthy. Is that a normal way to feel? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you unhealthy?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if I'd use the word *unhealthy*, necessarily. I hope that's an okay way to put it. I just... I think I'd feel sluggish, maybe? Like, my job as a painter, it's pretty physical. I'm up on ladders, reaching, bending... I just want to make sure I'm in the best shape to do my work well, you know? \n\nI guess I just associate being lighter with having more energy and being more efficient. I really don't want you to think I have some warped view of health or anything. I just try to take care of myself so I can keep up with my murals. Does that sound like a reasonable way to look at it? I just want to make sure I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is more about how you look or about something else?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's a tough one. I guess... I guess it's a mix of both? I mean, I know I talk a lot about how I look, and I really hope I don't sound incredibly vain when I say that. It's just... in my line of work, people see me, they see my art, and I just want to present a good image. \n\nBut I suppose it's also about... control? Or just keeping things in check. Like, if I have to go to a dinner with a client to discuss a mural, and maybe I have something with bread or something a little heavier than usual... if the scale goes up even just a tiny bit the next morning, I get... well, I guess \"tense\" is the right word. I just get a little anxious about it. So I'll usually just cut back on what I eat the next day, just to make sure it doesn't stick. \n\nI just like to keep things balanced, you know? I don't want things to get out of hand. Is that... is that pretty common for people to do? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to control your weight as much if you didn't care about how your body looks?\n\nPatient: Um... honestly, I think if I truly didn't care about how I looked, I probably wouldn't be quite as strict with myself. I know I'm sort of repeating myself here, and I'm really sorry to keep bringing it back to this, but it really just comes down to what I mentioned earlier about the mirror and the photos. \n\nLike, if someone posts a picture of me next to a mural I just finished, or if I just catch my reflection in a window while I'm working... I really do judge my entire day based on that. My whole mood just depends on whether my stomach looks flat or if my face looks sharp enough right then. \n\nSo I guess, if I didn't care about those things, I wouldn't feel this need to manage my weight so tightly. I just want to look presentable, you know? I hope it's okay that I keep going back to that same example. Does that answer what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still try to control your weight if you didn't feel like it was slipping out of control when you gain a little?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I guess if I didn't get that... that tense feeling, maybe I wouldn't be as strict? I just... I like to be proactive, I guess? I hope that makes sense. I've found that eating a full meal during the day really feels like it would slow me down, you know? Like I'd get sluggish going up and down the ladders. So I just keep it light to stay productive. Does that seem reasonable to you? I'm just trying to do what works best for my job, and I hope you don't think I'm being unhealthy about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... I wouldn't say I'm *afraid* of it, exactly. I hope that's the right word to use. It's more just... I like to keep things under control? \n\nLike, sometimes I have to go to a client dinner, or maybe I end up eating something with bread... and if I check the scale the next morning and it's gone up even just a little bit, I do get kind of... tense, I guess? So I usually just make sure to cut back the next day, just so it doesn't stick around. \n\nIt's just a way of staying on track with my routine. That's pretty normal, right? I mean, a lot of guys try to watch their weight as they get older, so I'm just trying to be responsible about it. I hope you don't think I'm being too extreme or anything.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I know I kind of just touched on this, but... I guess if you're asking again, maybe I wasn't being totally clear? I'm sorry about that, I really want to make sure I'm answering you properly. \n\nI guess... well, if I really think about it, maybe it is a little closer to a fear than I just made it sound. Like I was saying about those client dinners, or if I end up eating bread... when I step on the scale the next morning and see that it went up, even just a tiny fraction of a pound, I get really... well, highly anxious, I guess. It really bothers me. So I immediately start cutting back my food the next day to make absolutely sure it doesn't stick. I just really, really need that number to go back down to feel okay again. \n\nI hope I'm not sounding too crazy or difficult when I say that? I'm just trying to give you the full picture, like you asked.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is related to how you want to look?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little? I mean, I don't want to sound vain or anything. It's not like I'm obsessed with my looks, I promise. It's more just... wanting to look put together, you know? \n\nSometimes, if I catch a glimpse of myself in a mirror, or... or if a client takes a photo of me standing next to one of my finished murals—which they do sometimes, for their social media or whatever—I just... well, I kind of find myself focusing on my stomach or my face. And if they don't look... sharp enough, or lean enough, it just kind of ruins the whole day for me. \n\nI just want to look professional and healthy, you know? Like I take care of myself. I hope that makes sense. Is it okay that I care about that? I feel like a lot of people want to look good in photos, right?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight because you don't want to lose your current body shape?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess that's a big part of it. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm answering you right. \n\nLike I mentioned before about the photos... when I catch myself in a mirror, or if I see one of those pictures a client takes of me next to a finished mural... I really just zero in on my stomach and my face. And if they don't look exactly the way I want—if they aren't, you know, sharp or lean enough—it honestly just dictates my mood for the entire day. I end up judging myself so harshly based on that one thing. \n\nSo, yeah, losing this shape... it would mean I'd have to feel that way all the time, and I just don't think I could handle that. I just want to feel okay about how I look, you know? I've actually never thought about it like that before. I hope it's okay if I take a second to think about it? I just want to give you a good answer.\n\nI guess... if I really couldn't see myself at all... maybe the fear wouldn't be as bad? Because, like I was saying, so much of it is tied to what I see in the mirror or in those photos clients take. If I didn't have that visual... confirmation, I suppose, maybe I wouldn't be as anxious about it. \n\nBut then again, there's the scale, right? I'd still know the number. I'm trying to be as honest as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be afraid of gaining weight if it didn't affect your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess if it didn't change how I looked at all... maybe it wouldn't be quite as terrifying? I mean, I hope it's okay to admit that. I just... I know I've brought this up a couple of times already, and I'm really sorry if I'm sounding like a broken record, but it always comes back to what I see. \n\nLike I said about the mirror, or those pictures my clients post of me with my murals... I just can't help but judge my entire day, my whole worth, I guess, on whether my stomach or my face looks sharp enough in that exact moment. If my appearance didn't change, maybe I wouldn't have that visual trigger to make me feel so... inadequate? \n\nBut I still think I'd worry about the number, just because I want to be healthy. Is that a normal way to feel? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you unhealthy?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if I'd use the word *unhealthy*, necessarily. I hope that's an okay way to put it. I just... I think I'd feel sluggish, maybe? Like, my job as a painter, it's pretty physical. I'm up on ladders, reaching, bending... I just want to make sure I'm in the best shape to do my work well, you know? \n\nI guess I just associate being lighter with having more energy and being more efficient. I really don't want you to think I have some warped view of health or anything. I just try to take care of myself so I can keep up with my murals. Does that sound like a reasonable way to look at it? I just want to make sure I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is more about how you look or about something else?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's a tough one. I've found that eating a full meal during the day really feels like it would slow me down, you know? Like I'd get sluggish going up and down the ladders. So I just keep it light to stay productive. I'm just trying to do what works best for my job, and I hope you don't think I'm being unhealthy about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... I wouldn't say I'm *afraid* of it, exactly. I hope that's the right word to use. It's more just... I like to keep things under control? \n\nLike, sometimes I have to go to a client dinner, or maybe I end up eating something with bread... and if I check the scale the next morning and it's gone up even just a little bit, I do get kind of... tense, I guess? So I usually just make sure to cut back the next day, just so it doesn't stick around. \n\nIt's just a way of staying on track with my routine. That's pretty normal, right? I mean, a lot of guys try to watch their weight as they get older, so I'm just trying to be responsible about it. I hope you don't think I'm being too extreme or anything.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I know I kind of just touched on this, but... I guess if you're asking again, maybe I wasn't being totally clear? I'm sorry about that, I really want to make sure I'm answering you properly. \n\nI guess... well, if I really think about it, maybe it is a little closer to a fear than I just made it sound. Like I was saying about those client dinners, or if I end up eating bread... when I step on the scale the next morning and see that it went up, even just a tiny fraction of a pound, I get really... well, highly anxious, I guess. It really bothers me. So I immediately start cutting back my food the next day to make absolutely sure it doesn't stick. I just really, really need that number to go back down to feel okay again. \n\nI hope I'm not sounding too crazy or difficult when I say that? I'm just trying to give you the full picture, like you asked.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is related to how you want to look?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little? I mean, I don't want to sound vain or anything. It's not like I'm obsessed with my looks, I promise. It's more just... wanting to look put together, you know? \n\nSometimes, if I catch a glimpse of myself in a mirror, or... or if a client takes a photo of me standing next to one of my finished murals—which they do sometimes, for their social media or whatever—I just... well, I kind of find myself focusing on my stomach or my face. And if they don't look... sharp enough, or lean enough, it just kind of ruins the whole day for me. \n\nI just want to look professional and healthy, you know? Like I take care of myself. I hope that makes sense. Is it okay that I care about that? I feel like a lot of people want to look good in photos, right?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight because you don't want to lose your current body shape?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess that's a big part of it. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm answering you right. \n\nLike I mentioned before about the photos... when I catch myself in a mirror, or if I see one of those pictures a client takes of me next to a finished mural... I really just zero in on my stomach and my face. And if they don't look exactly the way I want—if they aren't, you know, sharp or lean enough—it honestly just dictates my mood for the entire day. I end up judging myself so harshly based on that one thing. \n\nSo, yeah, losing this shape... it would mean I'd have to feel that way all the time, and I just don't think I could handle that. I just want to feel okay about how I look, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound too superficial or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be afraid of gaining weight if you couldn't see your body?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's... that's a really interesting question. I've actually never thought about it like that before. I hope it's okay if I take a second to think about it? I just want to give you a good answer.\n\nI guess... if I really couldn't see myself at all... maybe the fear wouldn't be as bad? Because, like I was saying, so much of it is tied to what I see in the mirror or in those photos clients take. If I didn't have that visual... confirmation, I suppose, maybe I wouldn't be as anxious about it. \n\nBut then again, there's the scale, right? I'd still know the number. And I think just knowing the number went up would still make me feel... well, pretty uncomfortable. I'm sorry, I know that's not a very definitive answer. Does that make sense, though? I'm trying to be as honest as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be afraid of gaining weight if it didn't affect your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess if it didn't change how I looked at all... maybe it wouldn't be quite as terrifying? I mean, I hope it's okay to admit that. I just... I know I've brought this up a couple of times already, and I'm really sorry if I'm sounding like a broken record, but it always comes back to what I see. \n\nLike I said about the mirror, or those pictures my clients post of me with my murals... I just can't help but judge my entire day, my whole worth, I guess, on whether my stomach or my face looks sharp enough in that exact moment. If my appearance didn't change, maybe I wouldn't have that visual trigger to make me feel so... inadequate? \n\nBut I still think I'd worry about the number, just because I want to be healthy. Is that a normal way to feel? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you unhealthy?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if I'd use the word *unhealthy*, necessarily. I hope that's an okay way to put it. I just... I think I'd feel sluggish, maybe? Like, my job as a painter, it's pretty physical. I'm up on ladders, reaching, bending... I just want to make sure I'm in the best shape to do my work well, you know? \n\nI guess I just associate being lighter with having more energy and being more efficient. I really don't want you to think I have some warped view of health or anything. I just try to take care of myself so I can keep up with my murals. Does that sound like a reasonable way to look at it? I just want to make sure I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is more about how you look or about something else?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's a tough one. I guess... I guess it's a mix of both? I mean, I know I talk a lot about how I look, and I really hope I don't sound incredibly vain when I say that. It's just... in my line of work, people see me, they see my art, and I just want to present a good image. \n\nBut I suppose it's also about... control? Or just keeping things in check. Like, if I have to go to a dinner with a client to discuss a mural, and maybe I have something with bread or something a little heavier than usual... if the scale goes up even just a tiny bit the next morning, I get... well, I guess \"tense\" is the right word. I just get a little anxious about it. So I'll usually just cut back on what I eat the next day, just to make sure it doesn't stick. \n\nI just like to keep things balanced, you know? I don't want things to get out of hand. Is that... is that pretty common for people to do? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to control your weight as much if you didn't care about how your body looks?\n\nPatient: Um... honestly, I think if I truly didn't care about how I looked, I probably wouldn't be quite as strict with myself. I know I'm sort of repeating myself here, and I'm really sorry to keep bringing it back to this, but it really just comes down to what I mentioned earlier about the mirror and the photos. \n\nLike, if someone posts a picture of me next to a mural I just finished, or if I just catch my reflection in a window while I'm working... I really do judge my entire day based on that. My whole mood just depends on whether my stomach looks flat or if my face looks sharp enough right then. \n\nSo I guess, if I didn't care about those things, I wouldn't feel this need to manage my weight so tightly. I just want to look presentable, you know? I hope it's okay that I keep going back to that same example. Does that answer what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still try to control your weight if you didn't feel like it was slipping out of control when you gain a little?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I guess if I didn't get that... that tense feeling, maybe I wouldn't be as strict? I know I mentioned this just a minute ago, and I hope it's okay that I'm bringing it up again... but it's like what I said about those client dinners. \n\nIf I have something a little heavier, like bread, and then I see the scale tick up even just a fraction of a pound... I just get this urge to fix it right away. So I'll cut back the next day, just to make sure the weight doesn't stick around. \n\nIf I didn't get that anxious feeling about it slipping away from me, I suppose I wouldn't feel the need to course-correct so quickly. I just... I like to be proactive, I guess? I hope that makes sense. Is it normal to want to stay on top of things like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still try to control your weight if you felt completely in control of your eating?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's a really interesting way to put it. I guess... if I felt totally in control of my eating, maybe I wouldn't worry quite as much? \n\nBut honestly, I think I would still be pretty careful. I know I keep bringing up the same examples, and I'm really sorry if I sound like a broken record, but it really goes back to what I was saying about those client dinners. \n\nEven if I felt totally fine about eating something a little heavier, like bread, if I step on the scale the next morning and it's gone up even just a tiny bit... I just get that tense feeling again. I just want to make sure it doesn't stick around, so I end up cutting back the next day anyway. It's just... I like to keep things balanced, you know? \n\nI just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing. I hope it's okay that I keep giving you the same sort of answer. Am I giving you the kind of information you need?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is mostly about maintaining a certain body shape?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I suppose that's a big part of it. I know I'm probably sounding so repetitive today, and I really hope you don't mind that I keep circling back to this... but it really is just about wanting to look... well, put together. \n\nLike I mentioned before with the murals... if someone snaps a photo of me working, or if I just happen to see my reflection in a window... I really do let that dictate how I feel about my entire day. If my stomach doesn't look flat, or if my jawline doesn't look sharp enough right in that moment... it just throws me off. I just want to make sure I'm putting my best foot forward. I hope that makes sense?
